<!-- end lemma-29 -->
<!-- proof-38: body_md -->
Since $u\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{N})$ by Corollary \ref{cor:structure}, the assumed decay and $q\delta_{\sharp}>N$ give $m_{0}<\infty$, while positivity gives $m_{0}>0$. Choose $R_{5}\ge2$ so large that $\int_{B_{R_{5}/2}}u^{q}\ge m_{0}/2$. For $|x|\ge R_{5}$ and $|y|\le|x|/2$ we have $|x-y|\le3|x|/2$, hence
\[
W(x)\ge\int_{|y|\le|x|/2}\frac{u^{q}(y)}{|x-y|^{\sigma}}\,dy
\ge\Big(\frac{3|x|}{2}\Big)^{-\sigma}\int_{B_{|x|/2}}u^{q}\,dy
\ge A_{1}|x|^{-\sigma}.
\]
For the upper bound split $W=J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}$ according to $|y|\le|x|/2$, $\{|y|>|x|/2,\ |x-y|<|x|/4\}$ and the rest. Then
\[
J_{1}\le(|x|/2)^{-\sigma}m_{0},\qquad
J_{3}\le(|x|/4)^{-\sigma}m_{0},
\]
while on the second region $|y|\ge|x|/2$ and therefore
\[
J_{2}\le C_{\sharp}^{q}\Big(\frac{|x|}{2}\Big)^{-q\delta_{\sharp}}
\int_{|z|<|x|/4}|z|^{-\sigma}\,dz
\le C|x|^{N-\sigma-q\delta_{\sharp}}
\le C|x|^{-\sigma}.
\]
This proves \eqref{eq:Wsat-two}.
<!-- end proof-38 -->
<!-- lemma-30: body_md -->
\label{lem:Wmass}
Assume $u^q\in L^1(\mathbb R^N)$ and, for some $\delta>N/q$, $u(x)\le C|x|^{-\delta}$ for large $|x|$. Put
\[
m_0:=\int_{\mathbb R^N}u^q(y)\,dy.
\]
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Wmass}
|x|^{\sigma}W(x)\longrightarrow m_0
\qquad\text{as }|x|\to\infty,
\end{equation}
uniformly with respect to the direction $x/|x|$.
<!-- end lemma-30 -->
<!-- proof-39: body_md -->
Let $r=|x|$. Fix $M>1$ and split the integral defining $W$ into $|y|\le M$ and $|y|>M$. On $B_M$, one has
$r^{\sigma}|x-y|^{-\sigma}\to1$ uniformly in the direction of $x$, so the contribution tends to $\int_{B_M}u^q$. On the part $|y|>M$, split again according to $|x-y|\ge r/2$ and $|x-y|<r/2$. The first contribution is bounded by $2^{\sigma}\int_{|y|>M}u^q$, uniformly in $x$. On the second region one has $|y|\ge r/2$, hence, for $r$ large,
\[
r^{\sigma}\int_{|x-y|<r/2}\frac{u^q(y)}{|x-y|^{\sigma}}\,dy
\le Cr^{\sigma-q\delta}\int_{|z|<r/2}|z|^{-\sigma}\,dz
\le Cr^{N-q\delta}\longrightarrow0.
\]
First let $r\to\infty$ and then $M\to\infty$.
<!-- end proof-39 -->
<!-- theorem-7: body_md -->
\label{thm:resonant}
Let $N\ge3$, $p>1$ and $2p<\sigma<N$, and assume that $q:=p^*_{\sigma}>1$ and $\Lambda=0$; here $q>1$ is automatic. Let $u$ be a positive $D^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})$-weak solution of \eqref{eq:main}. Put
\[
a_{0}:=\frac{1}{p-q}.
\]
There are $c,C>0$ and $R\ge e$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:resonant-rate}
\frac{c(\log|x|)^{a_{0}}}{|x|^{\gamma_h}}
\le u(x)\le
\frac{C(\log|x|)^{a_{0}}}{|x|^{\gamma_h}}
\qquad\text{for }|x|\ge R,
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:resonant-grad}
\frac{c(\log|x|)^{a_{0}}}{|x|^{\gamma_h+1}}
\le |\nabla u(x)|\le
\frac{C(\log|x|)^{a_{0}}}{|x|^{\gamma_h+1}}
\qquad\text{for }|x|\ge R.
\end{equation}
Moreover $u$ is radially symmetric about some point $x_0\in\mathbb R^N$ and strictly decreasing in the radial variable.
<!-- end theorem-7 -->
<!-- proof-40: body_md -->
At $\Lambda=0$ one has $\gamma_{*}=\gamma_{h}$ and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:resonance-identities}
\sigma+(q-1)\gamma_{h}=N,
\qquad
q-1=\frac{N-\sigma}{\gamma_h}>0.
\end{equation}
Thus $1<q<p$. Moreover Lemma \ref{lem:exponents}(e) gives $q\gamma_h>N$. Set
\[
\gamma:=\gamma_h,
\qquad
\theta:=\frac{q-1}{p-1}\in(0,1),
\qquad
a_{0}:=\frac1{p-q}.
\]
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:a0theta}
a_{0}(1-\theta)=\frac1{p-1}.
\end{equation}
Choose
\[
\max\{\gamma_e,N/q\}<\delta_{0}<\gamma
\]
and write $\varepsilon_{0}:=\gamma-\delta_{0}>0$. Theorem \ref{thm:bootstrap} gives $C_{0}>0$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:res-start-upper}
u(x)\le C_{0}|x|^{-\delta_{0}}
\qquad(|x|\ge1).
\end{equation}
Hence Lemma \ref{lem:Wsat} supplies $A_{1},A_{2}>0$ and $R_{5}\ge2$ for which \eqref{eq:Wsat-two} holds. On the other hand, the Serrin--Zou lower bound \eqref{eq:lower} gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:res-start-lower}
u(x)\ge c_{0}|x|^{-\gamma}
\qquad(|x|\ge R_{1})
\end{equation}
for some $c_{0}>0$ and $R_{1}\ge1$.

\emph{Upper bound.}
Increase $\rho\ge\max\{R_{1},R_{5},e\}$ if necessary and set
\[
\delta_{k}:=\gamma-\varepsilon_{0}\theta^{k},
\qquad k=0,1,\ldots .
\]
Since $q\delta_{0}>N$, the saturated branch of $T$ is operative, and \eqref{eq:resonance-identities} gives
\[
T(\delta_{k})=\delta_{k+1}.
\]
We claim that there are constants $C_k>0$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:res-upper-k}
u(x)\le C_k|x|^{-\delta_k}\qquad(|x|\ge\rho)
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Ck-growth}
C_k\le M\theta^{-a_{0}k}
\end{equation}
with $M$ independent of $k$. The assertion for $k=0$ follows from \eqref{eq:res-start-upper}. If \eqref{eq:res-upper-k} holds, then by \eqref{eq:Wsat-two}
\[
Wu^{q-1}\le A_{2}C_k^{q-1}|x|^{-\delta_{k+1}(p-1)-p}.
\]
Put
\[
C_{k+1}:=\max\left\{
\left(\frac{A_{2}C_k^{q-1}}{h(\delta_{k+1})}\right)^{1/(p-1)},
\ \big(\max_{|x|=\rho}u\big)\rho^{\delta_{k+1}}
\right\}.
\]
Because $\gamma$ is the zero of $h$,
\[
h(\delta_{k+1})
=(p-1)\delta_{k+1}^{p-1}(\gamma-\delta_{k+1})
\ge c_h\varepsilon_{0}\theta^{k+1},
\qquad
c_h:=(p-1)\delta_0^{p-1}>0.
\]
The exterior comparison Lemma \ref{lem:comparison}, applied exactly as in Lemma \ref{lem:onestep}, gives \eqref{eq:res-upper-k} at level $k+1$. If
\[
D:=\left(\frac{A_{2}}{c_h\varepsilon_{0}}\right)^{1/(p-1)},
\qquad
B:=\big(\max_{|x|=\rho}u\big)\rho^{\gamma},
\]
then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Crec}
C_{k+1}\le\max\left\{D\theta^{-(k+1)/(p-1)}C_k^{\theta},B\right\}.
\end{equation}
Using \eqref{eq:a0theta}, one checks that
\[
a_{0}\theta k+\frac{k+1}{p-1}=a_{0}(k+1)-a_{0}\theta.
\]
Therefore, after choosing $M\ge\max\{C_0,B\}$ so large that
$D M^{\theta}\theta^{a_{0}\theta}\le M$, induction in \eqref{eq:Crec} proves \eqref{eq:Ck-growth}.

Now let $r=|x|$ be large and choose $k=k(r)$ so that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:kchoice}
1\le\varepsilon_{0}\theta^{k}\log r<\theta^{-1}.
\end{equation}
Then \eqref{eq:res-upper-k}--\eqref{eq:Ck-growth} give
\[
u(x)
\le M r^{-\gamma}\theta^{-a_{0}k}
\exp\!\big(\varepsilon_{0}\theta^{k}\log r\big)
\le C r^{-\gamma}(\log r)^{a_{0}},
\]
which proves the upper half of \eqref{eq:resonant-rate}.

\emph{Lower bound.}
For $b>0$ define
\[
\phi_b(r):=r^{-\gamma}(\log r)^b.
\]
If $L=\log r$, a direct radial computation, using $\gamma+1=(N-1)/(p-1)$, gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:log-plap-general}
-\Delta_p\phi_b
=H_b(L)r^{-N}L^{b(p-1)-1},
\end{equation}
where
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Hb}
H_b(L):=b(p-1)L^{-(p-1)}
(\gamma L-b)^{p-2}(\gamma L-b+1).
\end{equation}
Set
\[
b_0:=0,
\qquad
b_{k+1}:=\frac{1+(q-1)b_k}{p-1}.
\]
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bk}
b_k=a_{0}(1-\theta^k)\uparrow a_{0},
\qquad
b_{k+1}(p-1)-1=b_k(q-1).
\end{equation}
Choose $\rho$ still larger, if necessary, so that every $\phi_b$ with $b\in[b_1,a_0]$ is decreasing on $[\rho,\infty)$ and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Hbuniform}
0<H_b(\log r)\le D_{0}
\qquad(r\ge\rho,\ b\in[b_1,a_0])
\end{equation}
for some $D_0<\infty$. We prove inductively that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:res-lower-k}
u(x)\ge c_k|x|^{-\gamma}(\log|x|)^{b_k}
\qquad(|x|\ge\rho)
\end{equation}
with $c_k$ bounded below away from zero. The case $k=0$ is \eqref{eq:res-start-lower}, after reducing $c_0$ if necessary. Suppose \eqref{eq:res-lower-k} holds. By \eqref{eq:Wsat-two}, \eqref{eq:resonance-identities} and \eqref{eq:bk},
\[
Wu^{q-1}\ge
A_{1}c_k^{q-1}r^{-N}(\log r)^{b_k(q-1)}.
\]
Let
\[
\widehat c_{k+1}:=
\min\left\{
\left(\frac{A_{1}c_k^{q-1}}{D_0}\right)^{1/(p-1)},
\frac{\min_{|x|=\rho}u}
{\max_{b\in[b_1,a_0]}\phi_b(\rho)}
\right\}.
\]
For $R>\rho$ put
\[
v_R(x):=\widehat c_{k+1}
\big(\phi_{b_{k+1}}(|x|)-\phi_{b_{k+1}}(R)\big)
\qquad(\rho<|x|<R).
\]
By \eqref{eq:log-plap-general}--\eqref{eq:Hbuniform},
\[
-\Delta_pv_R\le A_{1}c_k^{q-1}r^{-N}(\log r)^{b_k(q-1)}
\le -\Delta_pu.
\]
Moreover $v_R=0\le u$ on $|x|=R$ and $v_R\le u$ on $|x|=\rho$. Lemma \ref{lem:comparison} gives $v_R\le u$ in the annulus, and letting $R\to\infty$ yields \eqref{eq:res-lower-k} at level $k+1$ with $c_{k+1}:=\widehat c_{k+1}$.

Put
\[
D_1:=\left(\frac{A_1}{D_0}\right)^{1/(p-1)},
\qquad
b_{\rho}:=\frac{\min_{|x|=\rho}u}
{\max_{b\in[b_1,a_0]}\phi_b(\rho)}>0.
\]
Then $c_{k+1}=\min\{D_1c_k^{\theta},b_{\rho}\}$. Since $0<\theta<1$, the map $t\mapsto D_1t^{\theta}$ has a positive fixed point $t_*$. Hence
\[
\underline c:=\min\{c_0,t_*,b_{\rho}\}>0
\]
satisfies $c_k\ge\underline c$ for every $k$. Letting $k\to\infty$ in \eqref{eq:res-lower-k} and using \eqref{eq:bk} gives
\[
u(x)\ge\underline c |x|^{-\gamma}(\log|x|)^{a_0}
\qquad(|x|\ge\rho),
\]
which completes \eqref{eq:resonant-rate}.

It remains to prove \eqref{eq:resonant-grad}. Let $R\ge2\rho$, put $L_R:=\log R$, and define on the fixed annulus $\mathcal A:=\{1/2<|y|<2\}$
\[
v_R(y):=R^{\gamma_h}L_R^{-a_{0}}u(Ry).
\]
The two-sided estimate just proved gives $\|v_R\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal A)}\le C$. Moreover, using \eqref{eq:Wsat-two},
\begin{align*}
-\Delta_pv_R(y)
&=R^{\gamma_h(p-1)+p}L_R^{-a_{0}(p-1)}
W(Ry)u(Ry)^{q-1}\\
&\le C L_R^{-a_{0}(p-q)}\le C,
\end{align*}
because $\gamma_h(p-1)+p=N=\sigma+(q-1)\gamma_h$ and $a_{0}(p-q)=1$. The interior gradient estimates of \cite{DiBenedetto1983,Tolksdorf1984} therefore give $\|\nabla v_R\|_{L^{\infty}(\{3/4<|y|<3/2\})}\le C$ uniformly in $R$. Scaling back gives the upper bound in \eqref{eq:resonant-grad}.

\emph{Lower gradient bound.}
We adapt the blow-down argument of Sciunzi \cite[Theorem 2.2]{Sciunzi2016}. Since $q\gamma>N$, the rate \eqref{eq:resonant-rate} implies $u^q\in L^1(\mathbb R^N)$; moreover it implies $u(x)\le C|x|^{-\delta}$ for some $\delta>N/q$. Hence Lemma \ref{lem:Wmass} applies. Suppose, by contradiction, that the lower bound in \eqref{eq:resonant-grad} fails. Then there are $x_n$ with $R_n:=|x_n|\to\infty$ such that
\[
R_n^{\gamma+1}(\log R_n)^{-a_0}|\nabla u(x_n)|\longrightarrow0.
\]
Set
\[
v_n(y):=R_n^{\gamma}(\log R_n)^{-a_0}u(R_ny).
\]
For every fixed annulus $0<a<|y|<A<\infty$, \eqref{eq:resonant-rate} gives
\[
c_A|y|^{-\gamma}\le v_n(y)\le C_a|y|^{-\gamma}
\]
for all large $n$. Furthermore, by \eqref{eq:resonance-identities}, $a_0(p-q)=1$ and Lemma \ref{lem:Wmass},
\[
-\Delta_pv_n
=(\log R_n)^{-1}\big(R_n^{\sigma}W(R_ny)\big)v_n^{q-1}
\longrightarrow0
\]
uniformly on compact annuli. The local $C^{1,\alpha}$ estimates therefore yield, by a diagonal argument,
\[
v_n\longrightarrow v_\infty
\qquad\text{in }C^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb R^N\setminus\{0\}),
\]
where $v_\infty$ is positive and $p$-harmonic in $\mathbb R^N\setminus\{0\}$. Passing to the two-sided bounds and then letting the annuli expand gives
\[
v_\infty(x)\asymp |x|^{-\gamma_h},
\qquad
v_\infty(x)\to\infty\ (|x|\to0),
\qquad
v_\infty(x)\to0\ (|x|\to\infty).
\]
By the $p$-harmonic rigidity theorem used by Sciunzi \cite[Theorem 2.1]{Sciunzi2016},
\[
v_\infty(x)=\alpha |x|^{-\gamma_h}
\]
for some $\alpha>0$. Let $y_n=x_n/R_n$. After passing to a subsequence $y_n\to y_\infty\in S^{N-1}$. The assumed failure of the gradient lower bound gives $|\nabla v_n(y_n)|\to0$, hence $\nabla v_\infty(y_\infty)=0$, a contradiction. This proves the lower bound in \eqref{eq:resonant-grad}.
<!-- end proof-40 -->
<!-- theorem-8: body_md -->
\label{thm:slowrate}
Let $N\ge3$, $p>1$ and $2p<\sigma<N$, and assume that $q:=p^*_{\sigma}>1$ and $\Lambda<0$. Let $u$ be a positive $D^{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{N})$-weak solution of \eqref{eq:main}. Then $u^q\in L^1(\mathbb R^N)$. If
\[
m_0:=\int_{\mathbb R^N}u^q\,dx,
\qquad
A_*:=\left(\frac{m_0}{h(\gamma_*)}\right)^{\!1/(p-q)},
\]
then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:slow-exact-u}
\sup_{|\omega|=1}\left|r^{\gamma_*}u(r\omega)-A_*\right|\longrightarrow0
\qquad\text{as }r\to\infty,
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:slow-exact-grad}
\sup_{|\omega|=1}\left|r^{\gamma_*+1}\nabla u(r\omega)+A_*\gamma_*\omega\right|\longrightarrow0.
\end{equation}
In particular, there are $c,C>0$ and $R\ge1$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:slowrate}
\frac{c}{|x|^{\gamma_{*}}}\le u(x)\le\frac{C}{|x|^{\gamma_{*}}},
\qquad
\frac{c}{|x|^{\gamma_*+1}}\le |\nabla u(x)|\le\frac{C}{|x|^{\gamma_*+1}}
\end{equation}
for $|x|\ge R$. Moreover $u$ is radially symmetric about some point $x_0\in\mathbb R^N$ and strictly decreasing in the radial variable.
<!-- end theorem-8 -->
<!-- lemma-31: body_md -->
\label{lem:slow-step}
Fix $\rho\ge R_{5}$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item Let $\delta\in[\gamma_{*},\gamma_{h}]$ and set $\nu:=T(\delta)$. If $u(x)\ge c|x|^{-\delta}$ for $|x|\ge\rho$, then $u(x)\ge c'|x|^{-\nu}$ for $|x|\ge\rho$, where
\[
c'=\min\Big\{\Big(\frac{A_{1}c^{\,q-1}}{h(\nu)}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p-1}},\ \big(\min_{|x|=\rho}u\big)\rho^{\nu}\Big\}.
\]
\item Let $\delta>0$ satisfy $q\delta>N$ and
\[
\gamma_{e}<\nu:=T(\delta)<\gamma_{h}.
\]
If \eqref{eq:Wsat-two} holds and $u(x)\le C|x|^{-\delta}$ for $|x|\ge\rho$, then $u(x)\le C'|x|^{-\nu}$ for $|x|\ge\rho$, where
\[
C'=\max\Big\{\Big(\frac{A_{2}C^{\,q-1}}{h(\nu)}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p-1}},\ \big(\max_{|x|=\rho}u\big)\rho^{\nu}\Big\}.
\]
\end{enumerate}
In (i), $q\delta>N$ by \eqref{eq:slow-order}, and $\nu\in[\gamma_{*},T(\gamma_{h})]\subset(0,\gamma_{h})$; in (ii), $h(\nu)>0$ is part of the stated range. Thus all barriers below are admissible.
<!-- end lemma-31 -->
<!-- proof-41: body_md -->
In (i), $q\delta>N$ by \eqref{eq:slow-order}, while this is assumed explicitly in (ii). Hence the saturated formula for $T$ is operative in both cases and gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:slow-exponents}
\nu(p-1)+p=\sigma+(q-1)\delta .
\end{equation}
Thus the two sides of every inequality below carry the same power of $|x|$ and no radius enters the constants.

(i) By \eqref{eq:Wsat-low} and $q>1$, for $|x|\ge\rho$,
\[
f:=Wu^{q-1}\ge A_{1}c^{\,q-1}|x|^{-\sigma-(q-1)\delta}
=A_{1}c^{\,q-1}|x|^{-\nu(p-1)-p}.
\]
Let $R>\rho$ and put $v_{R}(x):=c'\big(|x|^{-\nu}-R^{-\nu}\big)$ on $\Omega_{R}:=\{\rho<|x|<R\}$. Since adding a constant does not change the $p$-Laplacian, \eqref{eq:barrier} gives
\[
-\Delta_{p}v_{R}=h(\nu)(c')^{p-1}|x|^{-\nu(p-1)-p}\le A_{1}c^{\,q-1}|x|^{-\nu(p-1)-p}\le f
\qquad\text{in }\Omega_{R},
\]
by the first entry in the definition of $c'$. On $|x|=R$ we have $v_{R}=0\le u$, and on $|x|=\rho$ we have $v_{R}\le c'\rho^{-\nu}\le\min_{|x|=\rho}u$ by the second entry. Lemma \ref{lem:comparison} on the bounded domain $\Omega_{R}$, applied to the pair $v_{R},u$ in place of $u,v$, yields $v_{R}\le u$ in $\Omega_{R}$. Letting $R\to\infty$ gives $u\ge c'|x|^{-\nu}$ for $|x|\ge\rho$.

(ii) Symmetrically, by \eqref{eq:Wsat-two}, $f\le A_{2}C^{\,q-1}|x|^{-\nu(p-1)-p}$ for $|x|\ge\rho$. Put $v(x):=C'|x|^{-\nu}$ on $\Omega:=\{|x|>\rho\}$. Then $-\Delta_{p}v=h(\nu)(C')^{p-1}|x|^{-\nu(p-1)-p}\ge f$ by the first entry in the definition of $C'$, and $v\ge u$ on $\partial\Omega$ by the second. Since $\nu>\gamma_{e}$, we have $\nabla v\in L^{p}(\Omega)$, so $(u-v)^{+}\in D^{1,p}(\Omega)$ as in the proof of Lemma \ref{lem:onestep}. The exterior-domain assumptions of Lemma \ref{lem:comparison} are therefore satisfied, and it gives $u\le v$ in $\Omega$.
<!-- end proof-41 -->
<!-- lemma-32: body_md -->
\label{lem:slow-rigidity}
Let $1<q<p<2$, let $0<\gamma<\gamma_h$ satisfy
\[
\gamma(p-q)=\sigma-p,
\]
and let $m>0$. Suppose $V\in C^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb R^N\setminus\{0\})$ is positive and solves
\begin{equation}\label{eq:limit-slow}
-\Delta_pV=m|x|^{-\sigma}V^{q-1}
\qquad\text{in }\mathbb R^N\setminus\{0\},
\end{equation}
with
\[
c|x|^{-\gamma}\le V(x)\le C|x|^{-\gamma}
\qquad(x\ne0).
\]
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:slow-rigid-profile}
V(x)=A|x|^{-\gamma},
\qquad
A=\left(\frac{m}{h(\gamma)}\right)^{1/(p-q)}.
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-32 -->
<!-- proof-42: body_md -->
The function $U(x):=A|x|^{-\gamma}$ with $A$ as in \eqref{eq:slow-rigid-profile} solves \eqref{eq:limit-slow} by \eqref{eq:barrier}. The quotient $V/U$ is bounded above and below by positive constants. Set
\[
t_0:=\sup_{\mathbb R^N\setminus\{0\}}\frac{V}{U}<\infty.
\]
Assume $t_0>1$. Choose $x_k$ with $V(x_k)/(t_0U(x_k))\to1$, put $r_k=|x_k|$, and choose rotations $Q_k$ with $Q_ke_1=x_k/r_k$. The scale-invariance of \eqref{eq:limit-slow} gives
\[
V_k(x):=r_k^{\gamma}V(r_kQ_kx),
\]
which solves the same equation and satisfies the same two-sided bounds. Local regularity yields, up to a subsequence, $V_k\to V_\infty$ in $C^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb R^N\setminus\{0\})$. Since $U$ is invariant under the same rotations and scaling,
\[
V_\infty\le t_0U,
\qquad
V_\infty(e_1)=t_0U(e_1).
\]
On the other hand,
\[
-\Delta_p(t_0U)
=t_0^{p-q}m|x|^{-\sigma}(t_0U)^{q-1}
>m|x|^{-\sigma}(t_0U)^{q-1}
\ge -\Delta_pV_\infty .
\]
At this interior contact point, $\nabla V_\infty(e_1)=t_0\nabla U(e_1)\ne0$. Hence both gradients are nonzero in a sufficiently small neighborhood of $e_1$, and the strong comparison principle \cite[Lemma 2.7]{DaiLiLiu2025}, applied with $\tilde\Lambda=0$ in a small ball around $e_1$ to the pair $V_\infty\le t_0U$, gives $V_\infty\equiv t_0U$ near $e_1$. There, however,
\[
-\Delta_pV_\infty=-\Delta_p(t_0U)>m|x|^{-\sigma}(t_0U)^{q-1}=m|x|^{-\sigma}V_\infty^{q-1},
\]
contradicting \eqref{eq:limit-slow}. Thus $t_0\le1$ and $V\le U$.

For the reverse inequality put $s_0:=\sup U/V<\infty$ and assume $s_0>1$. As above, $-\Delta_p(s_0V)=s_0^{p-q}m|x|^{-\sigma}(s_0V)^{q-1}>m|x|^{-\sigma}(s_0V)^{q-1}$. The blow-down is again applied to $V$, along a sequence realising $s_0$, while $U$ is left unchanged because it is invariant under the rescaling. The limit satisfies $U\le s_0V_\infty$ with equality at $e_1$, where $\nabla U(e_1)=s_0\nabla V_\infty(e_1)\ne0$. Since $-\Delta_pU=m|x|^{-\sigma}U^{q-1}\le m|x|^{-\sigma}(s_0V_\infty)^{q-1}<-\Delta_p(s_0V_\infty)$ and $q>1$, the strong comparison principle gives $U\equiv s_0V_\infty$ near $e_1$, which contradicts the strict inequality exactly as before. Hence $U\le V$. Therefore $V\equiv U$.
<!-- end proof-42 -->
<!-- proof-43: body_md -->
The constants are chosen in the order $\delta_{\sharp}\to C_{\sharp}\to A_{1},A_{2},R_{5}\to\rho\to b,B$, and no later choice feeds back into an earlier one.

Since $q\gamma_{*}>N$ and $\gamma_{*}>\gamma_{e}$, we may fix
\[
\max\{\gamma_{e},N/q\}<\delta_{\sharp}<\gamma_{*}.
\]
Theorem \ref{thm:bootstrap} then provides $C_{\sharp}$ with $u\le C_{\sharp}|x|^{-\delta_{\sharp}}$ for $|x|\ge1$. Thus Lemma \ref{lem:Wsat} applies in full, and we fix $A_{1},A_{2},R_{5}$ accordingly. Let $R_{1}$ be the radius in \eqref{eq:lower} and set $\rho:=\max\{R_{5},R_{1}\}\ge2$, so that in particular $u\ge c_{0}|x|^{-\gamma_{h}}$ for $|x|\ge\rho$ with $c_{0}:=c_{1}$. Put
\[
b:=\big(\min_{|x|=\rho}u\big)\rho^{\gamma_{*}}>0,
\qquad
B:=\big(\max_{|x|=\rho}u\big)\rho^{\gamma_{*}}<\infty,
\]
which are positive and finite by continuity and positivity of $u$. Every exponent $\nu$ produced below by the lower iteration lies in $[\gamma_{*},\gamma_{h}]$ and every one produced by the upper iteration lies in $(0,\gamma_{*}]$; since $\rho>1$ this gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bB}
\big(\min_{|x|=\rho}u\big)\rho^{\nu}\ge b\ \text{ in the first case},
\qquad
\big(\max_{|x|=\rho}u\big)\rho^{\nu}\le B\ \text{ in the second}.
\end{equation}

\emph{The lower bound.} Set $\delta_{0}:=\gamma_{h}$ and $\delta_{k+1}:=T(\delta_{k})$. Since $T$ is affine with slope $\theta\in(0,1)$ and fixed point $\gamma_{*}$,
\[
\delta_{k}=\gamma_{*}+(\gamma_{h}-\gamma_{*})\theta^{k}\ \downarrow\ \gamma_{*},
\qquad
\delta_{k}\in[\gamma_{*},\gamma_{h}]\ \text{ for all }k .
\]
By the choice of $\rho$ we have $u\ge c_{0}|x|^{-\delta_{0}}$ for $|x|\ge\rho$. Define $c_{k+1}$ from $c_{k}$ by Lemma \ref{lem:slow-step}(i), so that $u\ge c_{k}|x|^{-\delta_{k}}$ for $|x|\ge\rho$ and all $k$. Let
\[
h_{\max}:=\max_{[\gamma_{*},\,T(\gamma_{h})]}h>0,
\qquad
\Phi(t):=\Big(\frac{A_{1}}{h_{\max}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}t^{\theta},
\]
so that $c_{k+1}\ge\min\{\Phi(c_{k}),b\}$ by \eqref{eq:bB}. The map $\Phi$ is increasing and, because $\theta<1$, it has a unique positive fixed point $t^{*}$ with $\Phi(t)\ge t$ for $0<t\le t^{*}$. Put $\gamma:=\min\{c_{0},t^{*},b\}>0$. If $c_{k}\ge\gamma$ then $\Phi(c_{k})\ge\Phi(\gamma)\ge\gamma$, and $b\ge\gamma$, whence $c_{k+1}\ge\gamma$. By induction $c_{k}\ge\gamma$ for every $k$, so
\[
u(x)\ge\gamma|x|^{-\delta_{k}}\qquad\text{for }|x|\ge\rho\text{ and all }k .
\]
Letting $k\to\infty$ and using $\delta_{k}\downarrow\gamma_{*}$ gives $u(x)\ge\gamma|x|^{-\gamma_{*}}$ for $|x|\ge\rho$.

\emph{The upper bound.} Set $\tilde\delta_{0}:=\delta_{\sharp}$ and $\tilde\delta_{k+1}:=T(\tilde\delta_{k})$, so that $\tilde\delta_{k}\uparrow\gamma_{*}$ and $\tilde\delta_{k}\in[\delta_{\sharp},\gamma_{*}]$ for all $k$; in particular $q\tilde\delta_{k}\ge q\delta_{\sharp}>N$, so the saturated branch is the operative one. Moreover, for every $k$, $\tilde\delta_{k}\ge\delta_{\sharp}>\gamma_{e}$ and $\tilde\delta_{k}<\gamma_{*}$ unless the iteration has already reached its limit, hence Lemma \ref{lem:Tprops}(e) gives
\[
\gamma_{e}<T(\tilde\delta_{k})\le\gamma_{*}<\gamma_{h}.
\]
Thus the assumptions of Lemma \ref{lem:slow-step}(ii) are satisfied at every finite step. Enlarging $C_{\sharp}$ we may assume $u\le C_{\sharp}|x|^{-\tilde\delta_{0}}$ for $|x|\ge\rho$, and we define $C_{k+1}$ from $C_{k}$ by Lemma \ref{lem:slow-step}(ii). With
\[
h_{\min}:=\min_{[T(\delta_{\sharp}),\,\gamma_{*}]}h>0,
\qquad
\Psi(t):=\Big(\frac{A_{2}}{h_{\min}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}t^{\theta},
\]
we have $C_{k+1}\le\max\{\Psi(C_{k}),B\}$ by \eqref{eq:bB}. Again $\Psi$ is increasing with a unique positive fixed point $s^{*}$ and $\Psi(t)\le t$ for $t\ge s^{*}$. Put $\Gamma:=\max\{C_{\sharp},s^{*},B\}$. If $C_{k}\le\Gamma$ then $\Psi(C_{k})\le\Psi(\Gamma)\le\Gamma$ and $B\le\Gamma$, so $C_{k+1}\le\Gamma$, and by induction $C_{k}\le\Gamma$ for every $k$. Hence $u(x)\le\Gamma|x|^{-\tilde\delta_{k}}$ for $|x|\ge\rho$ and all $k$, and letting $k\to\infty$ gives $u(x)\le\Gamma|x|^{-\gamma_{*}}$ for $|x|\ge\rho$.

Taking $c=\gamma$, $C=\Gamma$ and $R=\rho$ gives the coarse two-sided bound in \eqref{eq:slowrate}. Since the starting exponent $\delta_\sharp$ was chosen with $q\delta_\sharp>N$, we also have $u^q\in L^1(\mathbb R^N)$. Put $m_0:=\int u^q$. Lemma \ref{lem:Wmass} then gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:slow-W-exact}
R^{\sigma}W(Ry)\longrightarrow m_0|y|^{-\sigma}
\end{equation}
uniformly on compact subsets of $\mathbb R^N\setminus\{0\}$.

Let $R_n\to\infty$ be arbitrary and define
\[
v_n(y):=R_n^{\gamma_*}u(R_ny).
\]
The coarse estimate gives uniform upper and lower bounds for $v_n$ on every compact annulus. Moreover, by the balance identity
$\gamma_*(p-q)=\sigma-p$,
\[
-\Delta_pv_n(y)
=\big(R_n^{\sigma}W(R_ny)\big)v_n^{q-1}(y).
\]
Thus the local $C^{1,\alpha}$ estimates and \eqref{eq:slow-W-exact} yield, after passing to a subsequence,
\[
v_n\longrightarrow V
\quad\text{in }C^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb R^N\setminus\{0\}),
\]
where $V$ is a positive solution of
\[
-\Delta_pV=m_0|x|^{-\sigma}V^{q-1}
\]
and $V(x)\asymp |x|^{-\gamma_*}$. Lemma \ref{lem:slow-rigidity} therefore gives
\[
V(x)=A_*|x|^{-\gamma_*},
\qquad
A_*=\left(\frac{m_0}{h(\gamma_*)}\right)^{1/(p-q)}.
\]
Since the limit is independent of the chosen sequence $R_n$, the whole family converges in $C^1$ on compact annuli. Evaluating on the unit sphere gives precisely \eqref{eq:slow-exact-u} and \eqref{eq:slow-exact-grad}. In particular the two-sided gradient estimate in \eqref{eq:slowrate} follows. This is the same limiting-profile mechanism used by Sciunzi \cite[Theorem 2.2]{Sciunzi2016}, with the $p$-harmonic fundamental solution replaced here by the unique positive scale-invariant solution of the weighted subhomogeneous limit equation.
<!-- end proof-43 -->
<!-- lemma-33: body_md -->
\label{lem:tail-comparison-nonclassical}
Assume either the assumptions of Theorem \ref{thm:resonant} or those of Theorem \ref{thm:slowrate}.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item There exists $R_*>1$ such that $u\le u_\lambda$ in $\Sigma_\lambda$ for every $\lambda<-R_*$.
\item Let $I\subset\mathbb R$ be bounded. There exist a \emph{fixed} radius $R_I>1$, a number $\vartheta_I\in(0,1)$ and $C_I>0$ such that, for every $\lambda\in I$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:tail-with-remainder}
I_{1,\lambda}+I_{2,\lambda}
\le \vartheta_I(p-1)\mathcal E_\lambda
+C_I\int_{A_\lambda\cap B_{2R_I}}w^2.
\end{equation}
Thus the whole exterior contribution is absorbed with a uniform strict margin, while every interaction with the bounded region is kept explicitly in the last term.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-33 -->
<!-- proof-44: body_md -->
We treat the two phases separately.

\emph{The slow phase.}
Write $\gamma=\gamma_*$, $A=A_*$ and
\[
d:=N-p-(p-1)\gamma>0,
\qquad
s:=(2-p)(\gamma+1)>0.
\]
By \eqref{eq:slow-exact-u}, \eqref{eq:slow-exact-grad} and Lemma \ref{lem:Wmass},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:slow-tail-exact}
u(x)=A|x|^{-\gamma}(1+o(1)),\quad
|\nabla u(x)|=A\gamma |x|^{-\gamma-1}(1+o(1)),\quad
W(x)=m_0|x|^{-\sigma}(1+o(1)),
\end{equation}
uniformly in direction. If $\lambda\to-\infty$ and $x\in A_\lambda$, then both $|x|$ and $|x_\lambda|$ tend to infinity. Indeed, $|x|\ge|\lambda|$, while a bounded reflected point would contradict $u(x)>u(x_\lambda)$ because $u(x)\to0$ and $u$ is positive on compact sets. Since $|x_\lambda|<|x|$ in $\Sigma_\lambda$, the exact first asymptotic in \eqref{eq:slow-tail-exact} gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:reflection-ratio}
\frac{|x_\lambda|}{|x|}=1+o(1)
\end{equation}
uniformly on $A_\lambda$ as $\lambda\to-\infty$: otherwise a sequence with ratio bounded above by $1-\delta$ would give
$u(x)/u(x_\lambda)\le(1-\delta)^\gamma+o(1)<1$.
For $\lambda$ in a fixed bounded interval, the same relation holds geometrically on $A_\lambda\cap\{|x|>R\}$ as $R\to\infty$.

Since $q<p<2$, on $A_\lambda$ the mean value theorem gives
\[
W(u^{q-1}-u_\lambda^{q-1})w
\le(q-1)Wu_\lambda^{q-2}w^2.
\]
Using \eqref{eq:slow-tail-exact}--\eqref{eq:reflection-ratio}, for every $\varepsilon>0$ and sufficiently far in the tail,
\begin{align}
I_{1,\lambda}(A_\lambda\cap\{|x|>2R\})
&\le (1+\varepsilon)(q-1)m_0A^{q-2}
\int_{|x|>2R}|x|^{s-2}w^2,\label{eq:I1slow-tail}\\
M_\lambda^{p-2}
&\ge (1-\varepsilon)(A\gamma)^{p-2}|x|^s
\qquad\text{on }A_\lambda\cap\{|x|>R\}.\label{eq:weight-slow-tail}
\end{align}
Here $-\sigma-(q-2)\gamma=s-2$, which is equivalent to $\gamma(p-q)=\sigma-p$.
Apply \eqref{eq:cutoff-hardy} with $\ell=0$ to $w$. Its annular error is supported in $R<|x|<2R$ and will be retained in the bounded remainder. Since
\[
N+s-2=\gamma+d,
\qquad
m_0A^{q-p}=h(\gamma)=\gamma^{p-1}d,
\]
the limiting ratio of the exterior part of $I_{1,\lambda}$ to $(p-1)\mathcal E_\lambda$ is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:theta-slow}
\Theta_*:=
\frac{q-1}{p-1}\frac{4\gamma d}{(\gamma+d)^2}<1.
\end{equation}
Indeed $q<p$ gives $(q-1)/(p-1)<1$, while $4\gamma d\le(\gamma+d)^2$. Because the coefficient in \eqref{eq:cutoff-hardy} can be taken arbitrarily close to the sharp Hardy constant, the asymptotic errors and the cut-off loss can be chosen so small that the exterior part of $I_{1,\lambda}$ is bounded by $\vartheta_1(p-1)\mathcal E_\lambda$ with some fixed $\vartheta_1<1$.

For $I_{2,\lambda}$, Lemma \ref{lem:I2} gives
\[
I_{2,\lambda}
\le C\|u\|_{L^{p^*}(\Sigma_\lambda)}^{2q-p^*}
\int_{A_\lambda}u^{p^*-2}w^2.
\]
From \eqref{eq:slow-tail-exact},
$u^{p^*-2}\le C|x|^{-\gamma(p^*-2)}$ in the tail. Put
\[
\beta_*:=(p^*-2)\gamma+s-2=(p^*-p)\gamma-p>0,
\]
because $\gamma>\gamma_e$ and $(p^*-p)\gamma_e=p$. Hence on $|x|>2R$,
\[
u^{p^*-2}w^2
\le CR^{-\beta_*}|x|^{s-2}w^2.
\]
Splitting only the final weighted $w^2$ integral in Lemma \ref{lem:I2} into $B_{2R}$ and its complement, and then applying \eqref{eq:cutoff-hardy} to the latter, shows that, uniformly for $\lambda$ in a bounded interval,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:I2-tail-small}
I_{2,\lambda}
\le o_R(1)\mathcal E_\lambda
+C_R\int_{A_\lambda\cap B_{2R}}w^2.
\end{equation}
Thus no localization of the original double Hartree integral is being used. Here the factor $\|u\|_{L^{p^*}(\Sigma_\lambda)}^{2q-p^*}$ is simply bounded by the global $L^{p^*}$ norm. If instead $\lambda\to-\infty$, this factor tends to zero, so after $R$ has been fixed it supplies the required smallness for the starting position of the plane.

Combining the preceding estimates proves both assertions in the slow phase: for a bounded interval $I$, first choose one fixed $R_I$ so large that the sum of the exterior coefficients is strictly below $1$ and absorb every annular term into $C_I\int_{A_\lambda\cap B_{2R_I}}w^2$. For the starting position choose the same fixed radius and then take $\lambda<-2R_I$ sufficiently negative. Since $A_\lambda\subset\Sigma_\lambda\subset\{|x|>|\lambda|\}$, the bounded remainder then vanishes, and the factor in \eqref{eq:I2} is small; \eqref{eq:nonclassical-energy} forces $\mathcal E_\lambda=0$ and hence $w\equiv0$.

\emph{The resonant phase.}
Set $\gamma=\gamma_h$, $a=a_0$,
\[
s:=(2-p)(\gamma+1),
\qquad
\ell:=a(p-2).
\]
For $\lambda$ in a bounded interval, $|x_\lambda|/|x|\to1$ uniformly as $|x|\to\infty$. For $\lambda\to-\infty$ and $x\in A_\lambda$, both radii again tend to infinity, but the two-sided resonant estimate does not by itself give ratio convergence. What it does give, and what is sufficient, is the uniform comparability
\begin{equation}\label{eq:res-reflection-comparable}
\kappa\le\frac{|x_\lambda|}{|x|}\le1
\qquad\text{on }A_\lambda
\end{equation}
for some $\kappa>0$ once $\lambda$ is sufficiently negative. To prove this, otherwise there would be $x_n\in A_{\lambda_n}$, $\lambda_n\to-\infty$, with $t_n:=|x_{\lambda_n}|/|x_n|\to0$. The two-sided estimate \eqref{eq:resonant-rate} would give
\[
1<\frac{u(x_n)}{u(x_{\lambda_n})}
\le C t_n^\gamma
\left(\frac{\log|x_n|}{\log|x_{\lambda_n}|}\right)^a
\le C t_n^\gamma\bigl(1+\log(1/t_n)\bigr)^a\longrightarrow0,
\]
a contradiction. In particular
$\log|x_\lambda|/\log|x|\to1$ uniformly on the relevant tail. Together with \eqref{eq:resonant-rate}--\eqref{eq:resonant-grad}, this yields
\[
M_\lambda^{p-2}\ge c|x|^s(\log|x|)^\ell,
\]
and, since $q<2$,
\[
W(u^{q-1}-u_\lambda^{q-1})w
\le C|x|^{s-2}(\log|x|)^{\ell-1}w^2.
\]
The loss of exactly one logarithm is the identity $a(p-q)=1$. Applying \eqref{eq:cutoff-hardy}, now with the logarithmic weight, gives
\[
I_{1,\lambda}(\{|x|>2R\})
\le \frac{C}{\log R}\mathcal E_\lambda
+C_R\int_{A_\lambda\cap B_{2R}}w^2.
\]
Thus the local reflected term is arbitrarily small in the tail. For $I_{2,\lambda}$ we again use Lemma \ref{lem:I2}. Since
\[
\beta_h:=(p^*-2)\gamma_h+s-2=(p^*-p)\gamma_h-p>0,
\]
the factor $R^{-\beta_h}$ dominates the logarithmic powers. Splitting, as above, only the final weighted $w^2$ integral supplied by Lemma \ref{lem:I2}, and applying \eqref{eq:cutoff-hardy} to its exterior part, gives
\[
I_{2,\lambda}
\le o_R(1)\mathcal E_\lambda
+C_R\int_{A_\lambda\cap B_{2R}}w^2
\]
uniformly for $\lambda$ in bounded intervals. For $\lambda\to-\infty$ the $L^{p^*}(\Sigma_\lambda)$ factor in Lemma \ref{lem:I2} tends to zero. The same choice procedure as in the slow phase therefore proves (i) and (ii).
<!-- end proof-44 -->
<!-- lemma-34: body_md -->
\label{lem:local-continuation}
Assume either the assumptions of Theorem \ref{thm:resonant} or those of Theorem \ref{thm:slowrate}. Let $\lambda_0\in\mathbb R$ satisfy
\[
u\le u_{\lambda_0}\qquad\text{in }\Sigma_{\lambda_0}.
\]
If $u\not\equiv u_{\lambda_0}$ in $\Sigma_{\lambda_0}$, then there exists $\varepsilon_0>0$ such that
\[
u\le u_\lambda\qquad\text{in }\Sigma_\lambda
\quad\text{for every }\lambda\in[\lambda_0,\lambda_0+\varepsilon_0].
\]
<!-- end lemma-34 -->
<!-- proof-45: body_md -->
Choose a bounded interval $I$ containing $\lambda_0$ in its interior and let $R_I$, $\vartheta_I$ and $C_I$ be furnished by Lemma \ref{lem:tail-comparison-nonclassical}(ii). By the already proved upper gradient estimates and compact regularity,
\[
G:=\|\nabla u\|_{L^\infty(\mathbb R^N)}<\infty.
\]
Hence $M_\lambda\le G$ for every $\lambda$, and since $p<2$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:weight-global-lower}
M_\lambda^{p-2}\ge G^{p-2}
\end{equation}
wherever $M_\lambda>0$. On $\{M_\lambda=0\}$ one has $\nabla w=0$, so the convention in \eqref{eq:nonclassical-energy} gives directly
\[
\int_{\Sigma_\lambda}|\nabla w|^2
\le G^{2-p}\mathcal E_\lambda.
\]
The usual Sobolev inequality in the half-space, applied first to compactly supported approximations of $w$ and then by passage to the limit, gives
\begin{align}
\int_{A_\lambda\cap B_{2R_I}}w^2
&\le |A_\lambda\cap B_{2R_I}|^{2/N}
\left(\int_{\Sigma_\lambda}|w|^{2^*}\right)^{2/2^*}\notag\\
&\le C G^{2-p}|A_\lambda\cap B_{2R_I}|^{2/N}\mathcal E_\lambda,
\label{eq:small-measure-sobolev}
\end{align}
where $2^*=2N/(N-2)$.

By Lemma \ref{lem:strict-separation}, applied with $\lambda=\lambda_0$, the nonsymmetric alternative gives
\[
u(x)<u_{\lambda_0}(x)
\quad\text{for a.e. }x\in\Sigma_{\lambda_0}.
\]
Exactly as in the proof of \eqref{eq:classical-measure-small},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:A-measure-small}
|A_\lambda\cap B_{2R_I}|\longrightarrow0
\qquad\text{as }\lambda\downarrow\lambda_0.
\end{equation}

Combining \eqref{eq:nonclassical-energy}, \eqref{eq:tail-with-remainder} and \eqref{eq:small-measure-sobolev}, for $\lambda\in I$ close to $\lambda_0$ we obtain
\[
(p-1)(1-\vartheta_I)\mathcal E_\lambda
\le C_I C G^{2-p}|A_\lambda\cap B_{2R_I}|^{2/N}\mathcal E_\lambda.
\]
By \eqref{eq:A-measure-small}, the coefficient on the right is strictly smaller than $(p-1)(1-\vartheta_I)$ for $\lambda-\lambda_0>0$ sufficiently small. Hence $\mathcal E_\lambda=0$, so $w\equiv0$ and $u\le u_\lambda$ in $\Sigma_\lambda$. This proves the lemma.
<!-- end proof-45 -->
<!-- proof-46: body_md -->
By Lemma \ref{lem:tail-comparison-nonclassical}(i), the plane starts from $-\infty$ in every direction. Let
\[
\lambda_0:=\sup\{\lambda:\ u\le u_\mu\text{ in }\Sigma_\mu\text{ for every }\mu\le\lambda\}.
\]
At $\lambda_0$ we have $u\le u_{\lambda_0}$ in $\Sigma_{\lambda_0}$. If equality did not hold identically, Lemma \ref{lem:local-continuation} would move the plane past $\lambda_0$, contradicting the definition of $\lambda_0$. Hence
\[
u\equiv u_{\lambda_0}\quad\text{in }\Sigma_{\lambda_0}.
\]
Repeating the argument from the opposite side and in every direction yields radial symmetry about some point $x_0$. For strict monotonicity write $u(x)=U(|x-x_0|)$ with $U\in C^{1}([0,\infty))$, and note that $f:=W[u]u^{q-1}$ is then radial about $x_0$ and positive. Testing the equation with radial functions gives
\[
r^{N-1}|U'(r)|^{p-2}U'(r)=-\int_{0}^{r}s^{N-1}f(s)\,ds<0\qquad(r>0),
\]
the boundary term at $r=0$ vanishing because $U'$ is bounded. Hence $U'(r)<0$ for every $r>0$. This proves the remaining assertions of Theorems \ref{thm:resonant} and \ref{thm:slowrate}.
<!-- end proof-46 -->
<!-- remark-3: body_md -->
In the nonclassical phases, the symmetry proof is logically independent of the lower gradient bounds. Its new ingredient in the slow phase is the strict critical-tail coefficient \eqref{eq:theta-slow}; in the resonant phase the corresponding gain is the factor $1/\log|x|$. The role of the lower gradient analysis is instead to sharpen the asymptotic picture itself.
<!-- end remark-3 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
The proof uses $\Lambda<0$ through $\gamma_{*}<\gamma_{h}$, which guarantees $h(\gamma_{*})>0$ and keeps the power barriers of Lemma \ref{lem:slow-step} admissible. At $\Lambda=0$ this coefficient vanishes exactly at the limiting exponent; the logarithmic factor in Theorem \ref{thm:resonant} is the next-order correction to that degeneration. When $\Lambda>0$ the iteration is instead capped at $\gamma_h$, which is the classical Dai--Li--Liu phase.
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- proof-47: body_md -->
Theorem \ref{thm:local-boundedness} first gives $u\in L^\infty_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb R^N)$ under the assumptions of Theorem \ref{thm:three-regime}. If $\Lambda>0$, the assertions are exactly Theorem \ref{thm:main}. If $\Lambda=0$, they are Theorem \ref{thm:resonant}; in this case $q>1$ is automatic by Lemma \ref{lem:qbigger1}. If $\Lambda<0$, they are Theorem \ref{thm:slowrate}. Since these three cases are mutually exclusive and exhaustive, the proof is complete.
<!-- end proof-47 -->
